```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/spi-246 && gh pr create --draft --base sumeet/spi-243-pr2-cf-header-bridging --title \"Cross-FS: Stamp submitted_by + per-user job filtering (SPI-246)\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What this is\n\nThis PR makes every job in the system know who submitted it, and makes the GUI's job list show you only your own jobs (unless you're an admin, in which case you see everyone's). Today, every job in the database is anonymous — nothing on the row records who submitted it — and the GUI's \"active jobs\" view shows everyone's work to everyone.\n\nWith this PR, every job gets stamped with `submitted_by` at submit time, taken from the `User` that the CF ZT header bridging (PR #269) resolves to. The GUI list endpoints now filter by that field: non-admin users see only their own jobs, admins (the `admin: true` entries in `api_users.toml`) see everything automatically. There's no toggle and no extra UI element — admin status alone drives the unfiltered view, per the auth plan.\n\nThis is the second of three pieces of the demo-auth foundation. The run-cap ticket (next) will count against `submitted_by` at submit time to enforce the per-user `max_runs`.\n\n## Technical detail\n\nStacked on PR #269 (SPI-243 PR-2 — CF ZT header bridging). Base branch for review: `sumeet/spi-243-pr2-cf-header-bridging`.\n\n### Summary\n\n- **Schema**: new nullable `submitted_by VARCHAR(255)` column on `jobs`, indexed. Alembic migration `20260519_01` (head bumped from `20260518_01`).\n- **Contracts**: `JobAcceptedResponse.submitted_by` and `JobStatusResponse.submitted_by` added (both `str | None`, default `None`).\n- **Storage**: `DatabaseJobQueue.submit_job(submitted_by=...)` persists it; new `list_jobs(submitted_by=..., limit=50)` and updated `list_active_jobs(submitted_by=..., limit=10)` filter on it.\n- **Service**: `JobService.submit_research_job` / `submit_research_step_job` / `submit_research_batch_step_job` accept `submitted_by`, pass it down. New `JobService.list_jobs(submitted_by=..., limit=50)` returns `list[JobStatusResponse]`.\n- **Routes**: `POST /api/jobs/research` and `POST /api/jobs/research-steps` depend on `get_current_user` and pass `submitted_by=user.user_id`. New `GET /api/jobs` returns the current user's recent jobs (admins see all). `GET /api/jobs/active` now applies the same filter.\n\n### Approach\n\nThe filter rule: `submitted_by=None` to the queue layer means \"no filter\" (admin view); a concrete `user_id` string means \"only this user's rows\". Admins (via `User.is_admin`) get `submitted_by=None`; everyone else gets `user.user_id`. The route helpers `_submitter_id` and `_list_filter_for` encode this in one place so the same rule is applied at submit and at list.\n\nAdmin's own jobs **are** stamped with their `user_id` (e.g. `sumeet` in the example TOML). The admin flag affects the *list* view, not the *stamp* — so if an admin wants to see only their own jobs, that's a future filter parameter; today the rule is simply \"admins see everything.\"\n\nThe legacy `require_api_key` gate on the router-include stays untouched. Routes now have two layers of identity:\n1. `require_api_key` — the legacy gate on every router (`_api_auth = [Depends(require_api_key)]` in `services/api/app.py`). Returns `None` when `ELIJAH_API_KEY` is unset (dev-mode auth-disabled).\n2. `get_current_user` — new per-route dependency on jobs routes. Resolves the `User` via CF header or X-API-Key (per PR-2's logic).\n\nBoth run; FastAPI dedups identical `Depends`. SPI-247 will rip out (1) entirely once every caller is identifiable through (2). For SPI-246, layering keeps the diff small and the existing tests stable.\n\nAnonymous callers (auth disabled, or callers using the shared `ELIJAH_API_KEY` that resolves to the fallback `\"shared\"` user with no admin flag) leave `submitted_by=None` on submit and see the unfiltered list (since `current_user` is `None`). This is intentional for the demo deployment shape and matches what `require_api_key` would do alone.\n\nSource plan: PR #266.\n\n### Tests\n\n8 new tests in `tests/test_api_submitted_by.py`, 2 classes:\n\n`TestSubmittedByPersisted` (4 tests):\n- Submit stamps the resolved `user_id` from the CF header.\n- `JobStatusResponse` surfaces `submitted_by`.\n- Admin submissions are also stamped (admin flag affects list view, not stamp).\n- Unknown CF email auto-provisions and stamps with `user_id = email`.\n\n`TestListJobsFiltering` (4 tests):\n- Non-admin's `GET /api/jobs` returns only their own jobs.\n- Admin's `GET /api/jobs` returns all jobs (multiple submitters).\n- `GET /api/jobs/active` applies the same filter.\n- Auto-provisioned users see only their own jobs.\n\nAlso updated `tests/test_fs5_odds_multiplier_head.py::test_alembic_has_single_head_with_latest_storage_migration` to expect the new head (`20260519_01`).\n\nAn autouse `_reset_rate_limiter` fixture resets the slowapi limiter between tests (it's a module-level singleton; without this, multiple submits across tests share the per-IP counter and hit 429).\n\n### Verification\n\n- [x] `uv run ruff check .` — clean\n- [x] `uv run ruff format --check .` — 554 files already formatted\n- [x] `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend -x -q` — 1383 passed, 8 skipped\n  - Same transient `test_parallel_questions_faster_than_sequential` wall-clock-timing flake observed on every PR in this stack (asserts parallel execution < 0.6s; got 0.9s under load). Re-ran the file in isolation and all 11 tests pass cleanly. This refactor cannot affect parallel question-runner timing.\n- [x] `tests/test_api_submitted_by.py` runs green on its own (8 passed)\n- [x] `tests/test_api_auth.py` runs green on its own (44 passed)\n- [ ] Postgres-targeted migration tests — not run locally; the migration is straightforward additive column + index and uses the same `_column_names` / `_index_names` idempotency helpers as recent migrations.\n- [x] Frontend smoke test — not applicable; this PR is backend-only.\n\n### Risks\n\nLow-to-medium.\n\n- **Migration**: new column is nullable; existing rows get `NULL`. Filter rule treats `NULL` rows as belonging to \"no one\" — for non-admin views they're hidden, for admin views they show up. If anyone backfills `submitted_by` from `request_data` later, that's a separate one-off.\n- **List endpoint contract**: `GET /api/jobs` is new — no existing caller relies on it. Frontend will need a small update to render it, but no breaking change to existing endpoints.\n- **`GET /api/jobs/active`**: this endpoint *does* exist today. Today it returns all jobs; now it returns the current user's jobs (or all, for admin). If any deployed frontend currently shows the unfiltered list, it will need to either become admin-routed or accept the per-user view. Acceptable behavior change for the demo per the auth plan; flagging.\n- **Routes still gate on the legacy shared key**: by design (deferred to SPI-247). CF-authenticated callers and shared-key callers both work.\n\n### Branch-merge\n\nStacked on PR #269. Should not merge until PR-2 lands. After PR-2 merges into `dev`, rebase this branch onto fresh `dev` so its diff is just SPI-246's changes.\n\n### Out of scope (deliberately)\n\n- Run-cap enforcement — separate ticket (new run-cap ticket in the demo-auth epic).\n- LiteLLM-side per-user identity — SPI-244 (full-auth epic).\n- Removing the legacy `require_api_key` gate — SPI-247 (full-auth epic).\n- Backfilling `submitted_by` on existing jobs in deployed DBs.\n- Frontend changes to render the new `submitted_by` field or the new `GET /api/jobs` endpoint — separate follow-up (the API contract is in place; the GUI work can land independently).\n\nSource plan: #266\nStacked on: #269\n\ncc @kungfusaini\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open draft PR for SPI-246 stacked on PR-2"
}
```

| #269 | `sumeet/spi-243-pr2-cf-header-bridging` | Draft — CF header bridging + admin + `max_runs` |
| #270 | `sumeet/spi-246-submitted-by-and-filtering` | Draft — `submitted_by` + GUI filtering |

**Things SPI-246 introduces worth being aware of in review:**

1. **Behavior change to `GET /api/jobs/active`**: today it returns all jobs unfiltered; after this PR it filters by `submitted_by` for non-admin callers. The PR body flags this explicitly. If the existing frontend "active jobs" view relies on the unfiltered list, it'll start showing only the current user's work (or all, if admin). For the demo that's the desired behavior; flagging in case I'm missing context.

2. **`require_api_key` gate left in place**: routes still gate on the legacy shared-key dependency at the router-include level, *and* now additionally depend on `get_current_user` per-route. Per the plan, the wholesale swap lives with SPI-247.

3. **Migration idempotency**: `submitted_by` is a nullable add; safe for both fresh and existing DBs. Existing rows get NULL → invisible to non-admin filters; visible to admin views.

**Next up — the new run-cap ticket.** Want me to keep going right now, or pause for review on this stack?

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/run-cap/packages/auth/run_cap.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Per-user run-cap enforcement.\n\nCounts the user's \"in-flight or completed\" jobs and rejects a fresh submit\nwhen the count is already at the user's ``max_runs`` ceiling. The counted\nstates are decided at the call site (the storage layer owns what\n``count_counted_jobs`` returns); this module just compares the integer\nagainst the user's cap.\n\nThe rule:\n- ``user is None`` → no enforcement (auth disabled / shared-key fallback).\n- ``user.is_admin`` → uncapped.\n- ``user.max_runs is None`` → uncapped (typical for admin entries; also\n  applies to any TOML entry that omits the field).\n- otherwise: raise ``RunCapExceeded`` if ``current_count >= user.max_runs``.\n\nThe parallel-submit race is accepted; see the auth plan (decision #15).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\n\nfrom .registry import User\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)\nclass RunCapExceeded(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised when a submit would exceed the user's ``max_runs`` cap.\n\n    Carries enough structured detail for the API layer to translate into a\n    structured error response (HTTP 429, ``run_cap_exceeded``).\"\"\"\n\n    user_id: str\n    used: int\n    max_runs: int\n\n    def __str__(self) -> str:\n        return (\n            f\"User {self.user_id!r} has used {self.used} of {self.max_runs} demo runs.\"\n        )\n\n\ndef check_run_cap(user: User | None, current_count: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Raise ``RunCapExceeded`` if submitting one more job would exceed\n    the user's ``max_runs`` cap. Callers must pass the count of \"counted\"\n    jobs (queued + running + succeeded) for this user.\"\"\"\n    if user is None:\n        return\n    if user.is_admin:\n        return\n    if user.max_runs is None:\n        return\n    if current_count >= user.max_runs:\n        raise RunCapExceeded(\n            user_id=user.user_id,\n            used=int(current_count),\n            max_runs=int(user.max_runs),\n        )\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"RunCapExceeded\",\n    \"check_run_cap\",\n]\n"
}
```

  "old_string": "    def list_jobs(self, *, limit: int = 50, submitted_by: str | None = None) -> list[JobRow]:\n        \"\"\"Recent jobs, optionally filtered by submitter. ``submitted_by=None``\n        returns the unfiltered list (admin view); a concrete user_id returns\n        only that user's jobs.\"\"\"\n        with self._database.session_scope() as session:\n            stmt = select(JobRow)\n            if submitted_by is not None:\n                stmt = stmt.where(JobRow.submitted_by == submitted_by)\n            stmt = stmt.order_by(JobRow.submitted_at.desc()).limit(limit)\n            return list(session.scalars(stmt).all())",
  "new_string": "    def list_jobs(self, *, limit: int = 50, submitted_by: str | None = None) -> list[JobRow]:\n        \"\"\"Recent jobs, optionally filtered by submitter. ``submitted_by=None``\n        returns the unfiltered list (admin view); a concrete user_id returns\n        only that user's jobs.\"\"\"\n        with self._database.session_scope() as session:\n            stmt = select(JobRow)\n            if submitted_by is not None:\n                stmt = stmt.where(JobRow.submitted_by == submitted_by)\n            stmt = stmt.order_by(JobRow.submitted_at.desc()).limit(limit)\n            return list(session.scalars(stmt).all())\n\n    def count_counted_jobs(self, *, submitted_by: str) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Number of this user's jobs that count against their run cap.\n\n        Counted states are defined in ``RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES`` (queued,\n        running, succeeded). Failed / cancelled / partial / skipped jobs are\n        not counted.\"\"\"\n        with self._database.session_scope() as session:\n            stmt = (\n                select(func.count())\n                .select_from(JobRow)\n                .where(JobRow.submitted_by == submitted_by)\n                .where(JobRow.state.in_(RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES))\n            )\n            return int(session.scalar(stmt) or 0)"
}

Admins (and anonymous callers\n    when auth is disabled) get the unfiltered view; everyone else sees only\n    their own jobs.\"\"\"\n    if user is None or user.is_admin:\n        return None\n    return user.user_id",

Admins (and anonymous callers\n    when auth is disabled) get the unfiltered view; everyone else sees only\n    their own jobs.\"\"\"\n    if user is None or user.is_admin:\n        return None\n    return user.user_id\n\n\ndef _enforce_run_cap(user: User | None, job_service: JobService) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Raises an HTTP 429 with a structured ``run_cap_exceeded`` body if the\n    user has already reached their ``max_runs`` ceiling. No-op for admins,\n    uncapped users, and anonymous callers.\"\"\"\n    if user is None or user.is_admin or user.max_runs is None:\n        return\n    used = job_service.job_queue.count_counted_jobs(submitted_by=user.user_id)\n    try:\n        check_run_cap(user, used)\n    except RunCapExceeded as exc:\n        raise HTTPException(\n            status_code=status.HTTP_429_TOO_MANY_REQUESTS,\n            detail=ApiError(\n                code=\"run_cap_exceeded\",\n                message=(\n                    f\"You've reached your demo run cap \"\n                    f\"({exc.used} of {exc.max_runs} runs used). \"\n                    f\"Contact an admin to raise your limit.\"\n                ),\n                details={\n                    \"user_id\": exc.user_id,\n                    \"used\": exc.used,\n                    \"max_runs\": exc.max_runs,\n                },\n            ).model_dump(),\n        ) from exc"

RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES\nfrom services.api import create_app\nfrom services.api.rate_limit import limiter\nfrom tests.support.api_runtime import (\n    import_seed_json_into_database as Iterator[None]:\n    \"\"\"slowapi limiter is module-level — reset between tests.\"\"\"\n    limiter.reset()\n    yield\n    limiter.reset()\n\n\n_ALICE_EMAIL = \"alice@example.com\"\n_SUMEET_EMAIL = \"sumeet@sumeetsaini.com\"\n\n\nclass TestCheckRunCap:\n    \"\"\"Pure unit tests for the check_run_cap helper.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_no_user_means_no_enforcement(self) -> None:\n        check_run_cap(None, current_count=999)  # no raise\n\n    def test_admin_is_uncapped(self) -> None:\n        admin = User(user_id=\"sumeet\", name=\"Sumeet\", is_admin=True, max_runs=1)\n        check_run_cap(admin, current_count=999)  # no raise\n\n    def test_max_runs_none_is_uncapped(self) -> None:\n        user = User(user_id=\"alice\", name=\"Alice\", max_runs=None)\n        check_run_cap(user, current_count=999)  # no raise\n\n    def test_under_cap_passes(self) -> None:\n        user = User(user_id=\"alice\", name=\"Alice\", max_runs=3)\n        check_run_cap(user, current_count=2)  # no raise\n\n    def test_at_cap_raises(self) -> None:\n        user = User(user_id=\"alice\", name=\"Alice\", max_runs=3)\n        with pytest.raises(RunCapExceeded) as exc_info:\n            check_run_cap(user, current_count=3)\n        assert exc_info.value.user_id == \"alice\"\n        assert exc_info.value.used == 3\n        assert exc_info.value.max_runs == 3\n\n    def test_over_cap_raises(self) -> None:\n        user = User(user_id=\"alice\", name=\"Alice\", max_runs=3)\n        with pytest.raises(RunCapExceeded):\n            check_run_cap(user, current_count=4)\n\n\nclass TestRunCapCountedStates:\n    \"\"\"The counted-states set is canonical and documented.\"\"\"\n\n    def test_counted_states_are_exactly_queued_running_succeeded(self) -> None:\n        assert set(RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES) == {\"queued\", \"running\", \"succeeded\"}\n\n\ndef _registry() -> UserRegistry:\n    alice = User(\n        user_id=\"alice\",\n        name=\"Alice\",\n        email=_ALICE_EMAIL,\n        is_admin=False,\n        max_runs=3,\n    )\n    sumeet = User(\n        user_id=\"sumeet\",\n        name=\"Sumeet\",\n        email=_SUMEET_EMAIL,\n        is_admin=True,\n    )\n    return UserRegistry(\n        by_email={_ALICE_EMAIL: alice, _SUMEET_EMAIL: sumeet},\n        defaults=RegistryDefaults(max_runs=3),\n    )\n\n\ndef _build_app(tmp_path: Path) -> TestClient:\n    config = _seed_repo_state(tmp_path, api_key=\"\")\n    _import_seed_json_into_database(config)\n    app = create_app(config=config)\n    app.state.user_registry = _registry()\n    return TestClient(app)\n\n\ndef _submit_as(client: TestClient, email: str) -> tuple[int, dict]:\n    resp = client.post(\n        \"/api/jobs/research\",\n        json={\"question_ids\": [\"user_q_1\"]},\n        headers={CF_AUTHENTICATED_EMAIL_HEADER: email},\n    )\n    return resp.status_code, resp.json()\n\n\ndef _set_job_state(client: TestClient, job_id: str, new_state: str) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Direct-write a job's state for testing — bypasses the worker so we can\n    construct exact counted/uncounted populations cheaply.\"\"\"\n    job_service = client.app.state.job_service\n    queue = job_service.job_queue\n    with queue._database.session_scope() as session:  # noqa: SLF001 — test util\n        from packages.storage.database import JobRow\n\n        row = session.get(JobRow, job_id)\n        assert row is not None\n        row.state = new_state\n\n\nclass TestRunCapEnforcementAtSubmit:\n    def test_under_cap_submit_succeeds(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        code, _ = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n        assert code == 202\n\n    def test_at_cap_submit_returns_429_with_structured_error(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        # Fill Alice's cap (3 jobs, all in counted states).\n        for _ in range(3):\n            code, _ = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n            assert code == 202\n        # Fourth attempt is rejected.\n        code, payload = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n        assert code == 429\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"code\"] == \"run_cap_exceeded\"\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"details\"][\"user_id\"] == \"alice\"\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"details\"][\"used\"] == 3\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"details\"][\"max_runs\"] == 3\n\n    def test_admin_can_submit_past_their_max_runs(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        # Sumeet is admin and has no max_runs in this registry — even with\n        # many submitted jobs, the cap shouldn't fire.\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        for _ in range(5):\n            code, _ = _submit_as(client, _SUMEET_EMAIL)\n            assert code == 202\n\n    def test_failed_jobs_do_not_count(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        # Alice has cap=3. Submit 3 jobs, mark them all \"failed\" → counted goes\n        # back to 0. A 4th submit then succeeds.\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        ids: list[str] = []\n        for _ in range(3):\n            code, payload = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n            assert code == 202\n            ids.append(payload[\"job_id\"])\n        for job_id in ids:\n            _set_job_state(client, job_id, \"failed\")\n        # No \"failed\" jobs count → cap free.\n        code, _ = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n        assert code == 202\n\n    def test_cancelled_jobs_do_not_count(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        ids: list[str] = []\n        for _ in range(3):\n            code, payload = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n            assert code == 202\n            ids.append(payload[\"job_id\"])\n        for job_id in ids:\n            _set_job_state(client, job_id, \"cancelled\")\n        code, _ = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n        assert code == 202\n\n    def test_succeeded_jobs_count(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        ids: list[str] = []\n        for _ in range(3):\n            code, payload = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n            assert code == 202\n            ids.append(payload[\"job_id\"])\n        for job_id in ids:\n            _set_job_state(client, job_id, \"succeeded\")\n        # All 3 are succeeded → still counted → 4th is rejected.\n        code, payload = _submit_as(client, _ALICE_EMAIL)\n        assert code == 429\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"code\"] == \"run_cap_exceeded\"\n\n    def test_auto_provisioned_user_uses_defaults_max_runs(self, tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n        # Unknown CF email auto-provisions with defaults.max_runs (3 here).\n        client = _build_app(tmp_path)\n        for _ in range(3):\n            code, _ = _submit_as(client, \"newcomer@example.com\")\n            assert code == 202\n        code, payload = _submit_as(client, \"newcomer@example.com\")\n        assert code == 429\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"code\"] == \"run_cap_exceeded\"\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"details\"][\"user_id\"] == \"newcomer@example.com\"\n        assert payload[\"detail\"][\"details\"][\"max_runs\"] == 3\n"
}

The cap counts the\nuser's jobs in queued, running, or succeeded states; failed,\ncancelled, partial, and skipped jobs are excluded so the user isn't\npunished for pipeline-side problems or for jobs they cancelled.\n\nSurface:\n- packages.auth.check_run_cap(user, current_count) — raises\n  RunCapExceeded with structured detail when the cap is hit. No-op\n  for admin users, users with no max_runs, and anonymous callers.\n- packages.auth.RunCapExceeded — frozen dataclass exception carrying\n  user_id, used, and max_runs for the route layer to translate into\n  a structured HTTP 429 response.\n- packages.storage.job_queue.RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES — canonical\n  tuple of counted states (\"queued\", \"running\", \"succeeded\"). One\n  place to grep when the counted set is debated.\n- DatabaseJobQueue.count_counted_jobs(submitted_by) — SQL COUNT\n  over the user's jobs restricted to the counted states.\n\nRoutes:\n- POST /api/jobs/research and /api/jobs/research-steps run an\n  _enforce_run_cap helper after input validation, before submit.\n  Helper calls count_counted_jobs + check_run_cap and translates\n  RunCapExceeded into an HTTP 429 with body\n  {code: \"run_cap_exceeded\", message: \"...\", details: {...}}.\n- Helper short-circuits for admins, uncapped users, and anonymous\n  callers so the count query doesn't run unnecessarily.\n\nThe parallel-submit race is accepted (per the auth plan, decision\n#15): two simultaneous submits can both pass the count check before\neither has inserted, letting the user land at max_runs + 1. Worst\ncase at demo scale is one extra job; not worth a transaction or\nrow-level lock.\n\nTests:\n- TestCheckRunCap (6) — pure unit tests for the helper across the\n  no-user, admin, uncapped, under-cap, at-cap, and over-cap paths.\n- TestRunCapCountedStates (1) — pins the counted-states constant\n  so future changes are explicit.\n- TestRunCapEnforcementAtSubmit (7) — end-to-end via TestClient\n  covering under-cap, at-cap-returns-429, admin-uncapped,\n  failed-doesn't-count, cancelled-doesn't-count, succeeded-counts,\n  and auto-provisioned-uses-defaults.\n\nPer the auth plan (docs/plans/auth-plan.md, decisions #4, #11,\n#14, and the \"Scope of new run-cap ticket\" section), this\ncompletes the demo-auth epic.\n\nStacks on SPI-246 (submitted_by + GUI filtering).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -10",

```json
{
  "command": "cd /Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/run-cap && gh pr create --draft --base sumeet/spi-246-submitted-by-and-filtering --title \"Cross-FS: Enforce per-user run cap at job submit\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What this is\n\nThis PR is the third and final piece of the demo-auth foundation. With it in place, every non-admin user can submit up to their `max_runs` ceiling of jobs (3 by default for unknown CF-authenticated emails, configurable per user in `api_users.toml`). Once they hit the cap, the next submit gets rejected with a clean structured error — not a crash, not a silent failure — so the frontend can render \"Your max demo runs have been exceeded\" without guesswork.\n\nAdmins are uncapped (their TOML entries omit `max_runs`). Failed, cancelled, partial, and skipped jobs don't count toward the cap, so a user isn't punished for pipeline-side problems or for jobs they cancelled themselves. Queued + running + succeeded jobs do count — that's enough to prevent a user from sequentially queueing more work while previous jobs are still in flight.\n\nAfter this PR lands, the demo deployment has the full identity+attribution+cap loop: CF ZT identifies you, every job is stamped with who you are, the list endpoints filter to your work (or all if you're an admin), and the submit endpoints stop you at your cap.\n\n## Technical detail\n\nStacked on PR #270 (SPI-246 — `submitted_by` + GUI filtering). Base branch for review: `sumeet/spi-246-submitted-by-and-filtering`.\n\n### Summary\n\n- **`packages/auth/run_cap.py` (new)** — `check_run_cap(user, current_count)` raises `RunCapExceeded` when a submit would exceed the user's cap. No-op for admins, uncapped users, and anonymous callers.\n- **`RunCapExceeded`** — frozen dataclass exception carrying `user_id`, `used`, and `max_runs` for the route layer to translate.\n- **`packages/storage/job_queue.RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES`** — canonical tuple `(\"queued\", \"running\", \"succeeded\")`. One place to grep when the counted set is debated.\n- **`DatabaseJobQueue.count_counted_jobs(submitted_by)`** — SQL `COUNT()` restricted to counted states.\n- **Routes**: `POST /api/jobs/research` and `POST /api/jobs/research-steps` run `_enforce_run_cap(current_user, job_service)` after input validation, before submit. The helper translates `RunCapExceeded` into HTTP 429 with `{code: \"run_cap_exceeded\", message: \"…\", details: {user_id, used, max_runs}}`.\n\n### Approach\n\n**Counted states**: queued + running + succeeded, per the auth plan (decision #14, after the \"don't penalize failed runs\" amendment). The set is named `RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES` and exported from `packages/storage/job_queue` so the rule has exactly one home.\n\n**Short-circuit**: `_enforce_run_cap` checks `user is None`, `is_admin`, and `max_runs is None` *before* hitting the database. Admin and anonymous traffic doesn't pay a `COUNT()` query per submit.\n\n**Translation boundary**: `check_run_cap` is pure (no FastAPI, no DB). It raises a structured exception. The route helper catches it and shapes the 429. This keeps `packages/auth/` free of HTTP types — the auth package is reusable from the worker side too (relevant for SPI-244/245 in the full-auth epic).\n\n**Parallel-submit race**: accepted, per auth plan decision #15. Two simultaneous submits can both pass the count check before either inserts, letting the user land at `max_runs + 1`. Worst case at demo scale is one extra job; not worth a transaction or row-level lock. Real enforcement comes with SPI-245's LiteLLM-side dollar budgets.\n\n### Tests\n\n14 new tests in `tests/test_run_cap.py`, 3 classes:\n\n`TestCheckRunCap` (6 pure unit tests):\n- `user is None` → no enforcement.\n- Admin user → uncapped.\n- `max_runs=None` → uncapped.\n- Under cap → passes.\n- At cap → raises with structured detail (`user_id`, `used`, `max_runs`).\n- Over cap → raises.\n\n`TestRunCapCountedStates` (1 test):\n- Pins `RUN_CAP_COUNTED_STATES == {\"queued\", \"running\", \"succeeded\"}` so future changes to the counted set are explicit and reviewable.\n\n`TestRunCapEnforcementAtSubmit` (7 end-to-end tests via `TestClient`):\n- Under-cap submit returns 202.\n- At-cap submit returns 429 with structured body (`code`, `details.user_id`, `details.used`, `details.max_runs`).\n- Admin submitting past their nominal `max_runs` still succeeds.\n- Failed jobs don't count (3 failed + 1 new submit → 202).\n- Cancelled jobs don't count (3 cancelled + 1 new submit → 202).\n- Succeeded jobs count (3 succeeded → 4th rejected with 429).\n- Auto-provisioned user (unknown CF email) picks up `defaults.max_runs` as their cap.\n\nA helper `_set_job_state` direct-writes job state via the session so we can construct exact counted/uncounted populations without running the worker.\n\n### Verification\n\n- [x] `uv run ruff check .` — clean\n- [x] `uv run ruff format --check .` — 556 files already formatted\n- [x] `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend -q` — 1677 passed, 12 skipped\n  - Same `test_research_driver_pipeline_parallel.py` wall-clock timing flakes (2 failures) seen across the entire auth stack. Re-ran the file in isolation: all 11 tests pass cleanly. Run-cap changes cannot affect parallel question-runner timing.\n- [x] `tests/test_run_cap.py` — 14 passed on its own\n- [x] `tests/test_api_auth.py` + `tests/test_api_submitted_by.py` + `tests/test_run_cap.py` — 66 passed together (full auth stack)\n- [ ] Postgres-targeted migration tests — not run locally; this PR has no migration, only adds a SELECT-COUNT query.\n\n### Risks\n\nLow.\n\n- No schema change in this PR.\n- The cap is *only* enforced at the submit boundary. Once a job is in the queue, the worker doesn't re-check the cap. This is correct — in-flight jobs are already counted, so the cap is enforced on submit, not on dispatch.\n- If the count query becomes slow (it won't at demo scale — `submitted_by` is indexed and the user has at most a small number of jobs), it's behind a short-circuit that excludes admins and uncapped users, so steady-state demo traffic should hit it only for active non-admin users.\n- 429 vs 403 status code: chose 429 (\"Too Many Requests\") because the error is quota-shaped, not permission-shaped. The frontend should treat this as a soft block (\"you've used your runs\") rather than an auth failure.\n\n### Branch-merge\n\nStacked on PR #270. Should not merge until SPI-246 lands. After SPI-246 merges into `dev`, rebase this branch onto fresh `dev` so its diff is just the run-cap changes.\n\n### Out of scope (deliberately)\n\n- Frontend rendering of the `run_cap_exceeded` state. The API contract is stable (`code: \"run_cap_exceeded\"`, `details: {user_id, used, max_runs}`); the GUI work can land independently.\n- Dollar budget caps — SPI-245 (full-auth epic). Run-count cap is the demo's replacement.\n- LiteLLM-side per-user identity — SPI-244 (full-auth epic).\n- Resetting the count or refunding cancelled jobs in bulk — admins can already free a user up by raising their `max_runs` in TOML; that composes with this PR without extra code.\n- A `/api/users/me` endpoint exposing `{used, max_runs}` for the GUI to render usage. Likely worth doing for the demo so users can see their remaining budget; happy to follow up.\n\nSource plan: #266\nStacked on: #270\n\ncc @kungfusaini\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open draft PR for run-cap"
}

Run-cap PR opened as draft: **https://github.com/elijahintelligence/elijah/pull/272**

- Title: `Cross-FS: Enforce per-user run cap at job submit`
- Base: `sumeet/spi-246-submitted-by-and-filtering` (stacked on SPI-246)
- Branch: `sumeet/run-cap-enforcement`
- 5 files changed, +338 / −3
- 14 new tests (6 pure unit + 1 constant pin + 7 end-to-end)

**Full stack now:**

| PR | Branch | Status | Scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| #266 | `sumeet/auth-plan-doc` | Draft | Plan doc |
| #267 | `sumeet/spi-243-pr1-auth-package` | Ready for review | `packages/auth/` carve-out |
| #269 | `sumeet/spi-243-pr2-cf-header-bridging` | Draft | CF header bridging + admin + `max_runs` |
| #270 | `sumeet/spi-246-submitted-by-and-filtering` | Draft | `submitted_by` + GUI filtering |
| #272 | `sumeet/run-cap-enforcement` | Draft | Per-user run-cap at submit |

**Demo-auth epic is now functionally complete.** The flow from CF ZT → submit → cap → list works end-to-end across the four code PRs. After the stack lands on `dev`, the demo deployment will:
1. Identify each GUI user by their CF ZT email
2. Stamp every job with `submitted_by`
3. Show each user only their own jobs (admins see all)
4. Reject submits past `max_runs` with a clean 429 the frontend can render

**One thing I flagged in the PR-272 body that's worth your call:** a `/api/users/me` endpoint that returns `{used, max_runs}` would let the GUI show users their remaining budget *before* they hit the cap. Not strictly required, but a small follow-up that improves UX. Want me to do it as a tiny stacked PR?

Otherwise the demo-auth epic is done from the backend side; the only remaining work for the demo is frontend rendering (handling `run_cap_exceeded`, displaying `submitted_by`, the admin "see all" view) which isn't in the auth tickets' scope.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/users-me/services/api/routes/users.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Current-user surface for the GUI.\n\nExposes ``GET /api/users/me`` so the frontend can render \"who am I\"\n(identity, admin flag) and \"how many runs do I have left\" (used vs.\nmax_runs) without scraping other endpoints.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Depends\n\nfrom packages.auth import User, get_current_user\nfrom packages.contracts import CurrentUserResponse\n\nfrom ..dependencies import get_job_service\nfrom ..job_service import JobService\n\nrouter = APIRouter()\n\n\n_ANONYMOUS_USER_ID = \"anonymous\"\n\n\n@router.get(\"/users/me\", response_model=CurrentUserResponse)\ndef get_me(\n    job_service: JobService = Depends(get_job_service),\n    current_user: User | None = Depends(get_current_user),\n) -> CurrentUserResponse:\n    \"\"\"Identity and run-cap usage for the calling user.\n\n    When auth is disabled (dev mode, empty registry, no CF header) the\n    response shape stays the same with ``user_id = \"anonymous\"`` and zero\n    usage so the frontend doesn't have to special-case missing identity.\"\"\"\n    if current_user is None:\n        return CurrentUserResponse(user_id=_ANONYMOUS_USER_ID)\n    used = job_service.job_queue.count_counted_jobs(submitted_by=current_user.user_id)\n    return CurrentUserResponse(\n        user_id=current_user.user_id,\n        email=current_user.email,\n        is_admin=current_user.is_admin,\n        max_runs=current_user.max_runs,\n        used=used,\n    )\n\n\n__all__ = [\"router\"]\n"
}

{
  "command": "cd /Users/sumeet/matrix/elijah/.claude/worktrees/users-me && gh pr create --draft --base sumeet/run-cap-enforcement --title \"Cross-FS: Add GET /api/users/me for GUI usage display\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## What this is\n\nThis PR exposes the current user's identity and run-cap usage as a single endpoint so the frontend can show users how many runs they have left *before* they hit the cap, instead of guessing or only finding out when a submit gets rejected. It's a tiny follow-up to the demo-auth stack — one new endpoint, one new contract, no schema changes.\n\n`GET /api/users/me` returns the resolved user's `user_id`, `email`, `is_admin`, `max_runs`, and a live `used` count (number of the caller's jobs in counted states — queued, running, or succeeded). The count uses exactly the same logic as the cap enforcement, so what the user sees here matches what the cap allows at submit.\n\n## Technical detail\n\nStacked on PR #272 (run-cap enforcement). Base branch for review: `sumeet/run-cap-enforcement`.\n\n### Summary\n\n- New `packages/contracts/users.py` with `CurrentUserResponse` (user_id, email, is_admin, max_runs, used).\n- New `services/api/routes/users.py` with `GET /api/users/me`.\n- Registered under `_api_auth` in `services/api/app.py` so it sits behind the same gate as the rest of the API.\n\n### Approach\n\nThe endpoint short-circuits to a stable anonymous stub when `get_current_user` returns `None` (dev mode, empty registry). Rationale: the GUI shouldn't have to special-case missing identity; a stable `{user_id: \"anonymous\", used: 0, max_runs: null, is_admin: false}` response keeps the rendering code single-path.\n\nFor real callers it reuses the same `count_counted_jobs(submitted_by=user.user_id)` that the run-cap path uses, so the displayed \"used\" and the enforced cap can never drift.\n\nFor admin users, `used` is canonically *per-user* — i.e., the admin's own count, not the global list count they see at `/api/jobs`. Admins can have run quotas conceptually even if the demo TOML doesn't set one for them.\n\nSource plan: PR #266.\n\n### Tests\n\n6 new tests in `tests/test_api_users_me.py`, 2 classes:\n\n`TestUsersMeIdentity`:\n- Known email returns the registered identity (user_id, email, is_admin, max_runs).\n- Admin returns `is_admin: true` and `max_runs: null`.\n- Unknown CF email auto-provisions with defaults.\n- Anonymous fallback returns the stub when no registry / no CF header.\n\n`TestUsersMeUsage`:\n- `used` reflects submitted counted jobs.\n- Admin's `used` is their own count, not the global list count.\n\n### Verification\n\n- [x] `uv run ruff check .` — clean\n- [x] `uv run ruff format --check .` — 559 files already formatted\n- [x] `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend --ignore=tests/test_research_driver_pipeline_parallel.py -q` — 1674 passed, 12 skipped (skipped the known timing-flake file that fails on every PR in this stack)\n- [x] `tests/test_api_users_me.py` — 6 passed on its own\n\n### Risks\n\nLow.\n\n- Pure additive endpoint. No schema, no migration. Existing routes unchanged.\n- Anonymous stub behavior is intentional but worth flagging — in production where auth is required, anonymous won't appear because CF ZT will block unauthenticated traffic before it reaches this endpoint. In dev/local without auth, every caller is anonymous, which is the desired developer experience.\n\n### Branch-merge\n\nStacked on PR #272. Rebases onto fresh `dev` after the run-cap PR lands.\n\n### Out of scope (deliberately)\n\n- Frontend rendering of `{used, max_runs}`. The API contract is in place; the GUI work can land independently.\n- Listing all users (admin view). Different endpoint, different ticket if needed.\n- Editing the current user's settings. The TOML stays admin-edited.\n\nSource plan: #266\nStacked on: #272\n\ncc @kungfusaini\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open draft PR for /api/users/me"
}
```

> AGENT

`/api/users/me` PR opened as draft: **https://github.com/elijahintelligence/elijah/pull/273**

- Title: `Cross-FS: Add GET /api/users/me for GUI usage display`
- Base: `sumeet/run-cap-enforcement` (stacked on run-cap)
- Branch: `sumeet/users-me-endpoint`
- 5 files changed, +233 / 0
- 6 new tests; full baseline clean

**Final stack — demo-auth backend is complete:**

| PR | Branch | Status | Scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| #266 | `sumeet/auth-plan-doc` | Draft | Plan doc |
| #267 | `sumeet/spi-243-pr1-auth-package` | Ready for review | `packages/auth/` carve-out |
| #269 | `sumeet/spi-243-pr2-cf-header-bridging` | Draft | CF header bridging + admin + `max_runs` |
| #270 | `sumeet/spi-246-submitted-by-and-filtering` | Draft | `submitted_by` + GUI filtering |
| #272 | `sumeet/run-cap-enforcement` | Draft | Per-user run-cap at submit |
| #273 | `sumeet/users-me-endpoint` | Draft | `GET /api/users/me` |

**API surface the frontend now has:**
- `POST /api/jobs/research`, `POST /api/jobs/research-steps` — both stamp `submitted_by` automatically; both return 429 with `{code: "run_cap_exceeded", details: {user_id, used, max_runs}}` when the cap is hit
- `GET /api/jobs` — filtered for non-admins, all for admins
- `GET /api/jobs/active` — same filter rule
- `GET /api/jobs/{job_id}` — `JobStatusResponse` now carries `submitted_by`
- `GET /api/users/me` — `{user_id, email, is_admin, max_runs, used}` for header / budget display

That's the complete demo-auth backend. The only remaining work for the demo is frontend rendering (handling `run_cap_exceeded`, displaying `submitted_by`, the admin "see all" view, and the `/users/me` usage indicator). Backend-side, the demo-auth epic is done.

> AGENT

Found it — PR-1 (#267) landed on `dev` (squash-merged as `4e2de9d`), and there's a new commit `#276` on `dev` after that. PR-2's branch still has its original PR-1 commit (different SHA than the squash). Result: git sees them as conflicting versions of the same files.

This is the expected "stacked PR after parent merges" situation. The fix is to rebase PR-2 onto current `dev`, which drops the original PR-1 commit (its tree is already in dev) and leaves only PR-2's own changes on top. After rebase I'd force-push.

Before doing it, want me to:

1. **Inspect what would conflict** first (read-only `git merge-tree`) so we know the scope, then rebase + force-push if it's clean?
2. **Just do the rebase** (it's the normal flow, and CLAUDE.md explicitly allows force-pushing to feature branches after rebase as long as I tell you)?

I'd lean #2 since stacked PRs always go through this once their parent lands — happy to do #1 if you want to see the conflict first.

Also worth noting: after PR-2 rebases, **#270, #272, and #273 will need the same rebase treatment** (they're all stacked downstream). If you're OK with #2, I'll cascade the rebase through all four.

> DEVELOPER

im confused what the current stae is what's thhe issue?

> AGENT

Let me explain simply.

### What's the current state?

PR-1 (#267, the auth-package refactor) **has been merged into `dev`**. So the `packages/auth/` directory now exists on `dev`.

But PR-2 (#269) is still open as a draft. PR-2's branch still has its original copy of PR-1's commit baked into its history (because PR-2 was branched off PR-1 before PR-1 merged).

### Why git is upset

When PR-1 got merged, GitHub used a "squash merge" — that smushes all of PR-1's commits into **one new commit on `dev` with a brand new SHA**. The actual file changes are identical, but git only knows two things:
- PR-2 modified `packages/auth/registry.py`
- `dev` modified `packages/auth/registry.py`
- The commits don't share a SHA, so git thinks they're different changes that need merging

So PR-2 shows "conflicting" even though the *content* is identical. It's a paperwork problem, not a real merge issue.

### What CI says

CI is green on PR-2 itself — lint, test, docker-smoke all passed. The "conflicts" are purely the git-history mismatch with `dev`, not failing tests.

### The fix

Rebase PR-2's branch onto current `dev`. That tells git "throw away the duplicate PR-1 commit from PR-2's history because it's already on `dev`." After the rebase, PR-2's branch only contains its own real changes (CF header bridging, admin, max_runs, the email scrub).

This is the normal pattern for stacked PRs — every time a parent merges, the children need a rebase to "catch up" to the new `dev`.

### What I'd do

1. Rebase PR-2 onto fresh `dev`, force-push (force-push is required because rebase rewrites history)
2. Cascade the same rebase through PR-3 (#270), PR-4 (#272), and PR-5 (#273) since they're all stacked downstream

Does that make sense? Want me to go ahead?
